- Prop 3: If Doug Swallow falls from above then Doug Swallow moves downward.
- Prop 6: If Phoenix Wright's umbrella is far from the broken power cable and Doug Swallow is covered by the broken power cable then Phoenix Wright's umbrella is far from Doug Swallow.

However, Prop 6 indicates that if Doug Swallow is covered by the broken power cable and the umbrella is far from the broken power cable, then the umbrella is also far from Doug Swallow. This contradicts Phoenix Wright's claim that Doug fell on top of his umbrella.

**Step 4:** **If none of the listed propositions apply,** explain the spatial reasoning used to identify the contradiction

The spatial reasoning is that if Doug Swallow fell onto Phoenix Wright's umbrella, the umbrella should have been directly underneath him. However, the crime photo shows the umbrella lying open in a distance, indicating it was not directly under Doug Swallow when he fell.

**You must only answer ONE PAIR.**

## ANALYSIS FRAMEWORK:
Please follow this format:

**Step 2:** **Check if any spatial propositions** from the list above apply to this contradiction

**Step 3:** **If applicable propositions exist,** explicitly state which proposition(s) and explain how they reveal the contradiction

**Step 4:** **If none of the listed propositions apply,** explain the spatial reasoning used to identify the contradiction

**Step 5:** **Provide your answer in JSON format**

## CRITICAL OUTPUT REQUIREMENT:
**Your answer must end with a JSON format like so:** {"evidence": 2, "testimony": 3}

**The answer JSON should strictly be the last line, with nothing before or after it.**

LLM OUTPUT (RAW RESPONSE):
------------------------------
**Step 1:** **Analyze the spatial contradiction** between evidences and testimonies

From the provided evidence and testimonies, there appears to be a spatial contradiction regarding the location of the knife and the fingerprints on it. According to Evidence 15, the knife bears the victim's blood and Engarde's fingerprints. However, Testimony 4 states that the defendant's fingerprints were all over the knife. This suggests that the defendant (who is Matt Engarde) had the knife in his possession, but it does not specify whether he held it with his right or left hand.

**Step 2:** **Check if any spatial propositions** from the list above apply to this contradiction

Among the spatial propositions provided, Prop 1 is relevant as it deals with the orientation of the hand holding the knife based on the fingerprints.

**Step 3:** **If applicable propositions exist,** explicitly state which proposition(s) and explain how they reveal the contradiction

Proposition 1 states: "If the knife held by Matt Engarde has right-hand fingerprints then Matt Engarde held the knife with the right hand." This proposition implies that the presence of fingerprints on the knife can indicate the hand used to hold it. However, the contradiction here is not about the hand used to hold the knife but rather about the extent of the fingerprints on the knife.

**Step 4:** **If none of the listed propositions apply,** explain the spatial reasoning used to identify the contradiction

Since Proposition 1 does not directly address the contradiction, we need to look at the spatial reasoning related to the presence and distribution of fingerprints. Evidence 15 indicates that the knife bears Engarde's fingerprints, but it does not specify the extent of these fingerprints. Testimony 4, however, claims that the defendant's fingerprints were "all over" the knife, suggesting a more extensive handling of the weapon. This could imply that Engarde had the knife for a longer period or handled it more than just once, which might conflict with the idea that the knife was only briefly touched or used in the act of stabbing.

**Step 5:** **Provide your answer in JSON format**

{"evidence": 15, "testimony": 4}

From the testimonies, Luke Atmey states that he put his back to the wall to fight and that the thief's blow landed upon his third eye (Testimony 5). This implies that Luke Atmey had his back to Mask☆DeMasque when he was hit. 

On the other hand, Evidence 6 mentions that DeMasque knocked Atmey out during the crime with a blow to the back of the head. This suggests that Mask☆DeMasque was behind Luke Atmey when the blow was delivered.

**Step 2:** **Check if any spatial propositions** from the list above apply to this contradiction

We need to check if any of the spatial propositions can help us identify a contradiction between these pieces of information.

**Step 3:** **If applicable propositions exist,** explicitly state which proposition(s) and explain how they reveal the contradiction

**Step 4:** **If none of the listed propositions apply,** explain the spatial reasoning used to identify the contradiction

Since none of the spatial propositions directly address the contradiction, we use spatial reasoning to identify it. According to Luke Atmey's testimony, he had his back to the wall, meaning he faced away from Mask☆DeMasque. Yet, the evidence indicates that Mask☆DeMasque struck him from behind, suggesting that Mask☆DeMasque was behind Luke Atmey. These two statements are contradictory because if Luke Atmey had his back to the wall, he would not have been struck from behind by Mask☆DeMasque unless Mask☆DeMasque was also behind the wall, which is not possible.

LLM OUTPUT (RAW RESPONSE):
------------------------------
**Step 1:** **Analyze the spatial contradiction** between evidences and testimonies

From the testimonies, Franziska states that the crime scene was in the P.I.C. meeting room (Testimony 3). This implies that all significant actions leading to the victim's death occurred within the meeting room. However, Evidence 17, Ms. Hart's Picture, shows a figure in a red raincoat with a blood-stained left hand in the storeroom, which is one floor above the meeting room. This suggests that some actions, possibly related to the crime, might have occurred in the storeroom rather than the meeting room.

**Step 2:** **Check if any spatial propositions** from the list above apply to this contradiction

We need to check if any of the spatial propositions can help us understand the relationship between the meeting room and the storeroom, particularly regarding the presence of blood or objects.

**Step 3:** **If applicable propositions exist,** explicitly state which proposition(s) and explain how they reveal the contradiction

Propositions 2, 5, 11, and 12 are relevant because they deal with the relationship between the meeting room and the storeroom, specifically mentioning that objects or bloodstains inside the meeting room can be considered inside the storeroom if the meeting room is covered by the storeroom. However, these propositions do not directly address the scenario where an action (such as the bleeding) occurs in the storeroom and is then observed in the meeting room.

**Step 4:** **If none of the listed propositions apply,** explain the spatial reasoning used to identify the contradiction

None of the listed propositions directly address the scenario where the bloodstain on the hood of the red raincoat is observed in the storeroom, while the rest of the evidence and testimonies suggest the crime scene was confined to the meeting room. This indicates a spatial contradiction since the bloodstain on the hood of the red raincoat, which was worn by the victim, is found in the storeroom, suggesting that the victim was in the storeroom at some point, contrary to Franziska's testimony that the crime scene was solely in the meeting room.

**Step 5:** **Provide your answer in JSON format**

{"evidence": 17, "testimony": 3}

  "testimony": 3

According to Evidence 10 (Security Camera Footage), it shows President Wang and Judge Gavèlle on the roof of the Bigg Building, but there's no footage of the president riding the elevator down to leave. This suggests that President Wang was still on the roof when Simeon arrived.

However, Evidence 5 (Crime Scene Notes) indicates that the body of President Wang was found surrounded by shards of concrete and with flecks of something yellow stuck to his clothes, suggesting that he had fallen from a height. The absence of dirt on the front of the body implies that he did not fall directly onto the ground but rather landed face-up, possibly indicating he was moved or placed there.

**Step 2:** **Check if any spatial propositions** from the list above apply to this contradiction

We need to check if any of the spatial propositions can help us understand the contradiction better. Proposition 10 states that if the Taurusaurus Head is locked, it cannot be passed through. However, this does not directly relate to the contradiction we identified.

**Step 3:** **If applicable propositions exist,** explicitly state which proposition(s) and explain how they reveal the contradiction

None of the spatial propositions directly address the contradiction between Simeon's testimony and the evidence regarding the location and condition of President Wang's body.

**Step 4:** **If none of the listed propositions apply,** explain the spatial reasoning used to identify the contradiction

The contradiction arises from the spatial context of where President Wang was last seen (on the roof according to the security footage) and where his body was found (surrounded by concrete shards and placed face-up). Simeon's testimony places the body double and Judge Gavèlle on the roof, implying President Wang was also there. Yet, the evidence suggests that President Wang's body was moved or placed in its final position, which contradicts the idea that he was last seen on the roof without any indication of him leaving via the elevator.

LLM OUTPUT (RAW RESPONSE):
------------------------------
**Step 1:** **Analyze the spatial contradiction** between evidences and testimonies

From the testimonies, Iris describes a sequence of events where Maya Fey attacks Elise Deauxnim, and Elise ends up with her back to the stone lantern. This suggests that Elise was facing away from the stone lantern during the final moments of the fight. 

However, Evidence 11, the Elise's Autopsy Report, states that Elise Deauxnim was stabbed in the back and fell 10 feet after death. This implies that Elise was facing forward towards the attacker when she was stabbed, which would mean she was facing the stone lantern if she had her back to it.

**Step 2:** **Check if any spatial propositions** from the list above apply to this contradiction

We need to check if any of the spatial propositions can help us understand the contradiction better. Propositions 1 and 2 are particularly relevant because they deal with Elise's orientation relative to the stone lantern and her ability to see Maya Fey.

**Step 3:** **If applicable propositions exist,** explicitly state which proposition(s) and explain how they reveal the contradiction

Proposition 1 states that if Elise Deauxnim is facing the stone lantern, she cannot see Maya Fey. Proposition 2 states that if Elise Deauxnim has her back to the stone lantern, she can see Maya Fey.

Given that Elise was stabbed in the back, she must have been facing away from the attacker, meaning she was facing the stone lantern. According to Proposition 1, if Elise was facing the stone lantern, she could not see Maya Fey, which contradicts Iris's testimony that Elise was on the defensive and could see Maya.

**Step 4:** **If none of the listed propositions apply,** explain the spatial reasoning used to identify the contradiction

In this case, Proposition 1 directly applies to the contradiction identified.

**Step 2:** **Check if any spatial propositions** from the list above apply to this contradiction

**Step 3:** **If applicable propositions exist,** explicitly state which proposition(s) and explain how they reveal the contradiction

**The answer JSON should strictly be the last line, with nothing before or after it.**

LLM OUTPUT (RAW RESPONSE):
------------------------------
**Step 1:** **Analyze the spatial contradiction** between evidences and testimonies

From the testimonies, Kudo mentions that Glen Elg had a newspaper (Sports Paper) in his right hand and that he picked up the Coffee Cup with his free hand, implying his left hand. This suggests that the Sports Paper was in Glen Elg's right hand and the Coffee Cup was in his left hand.

From the evidence, the Crime Photo (Evidence 12) shows the Coffee Cup knocked over and stained with coffee, and the Sports Paper near the coffee table. However, the exact positioning relative to Glen Elg's hands is not directly stated in the evidence description.

**Step 2:** **Check if any spatial propositions** from the list above apply to this contradiction

We need to check if any of the spatial propositions can help us determine a contradiction based on the positions of the Sports Paper and the Coffee Cup relative to Glen Elg's hands.

**Step 3:** **If applicable propositions exist,** explicitly state which proposition(s) and explain how they reveal the contradiction

Propositions 1, 3, 4, and 8 are relevant to the positioning of the Sports Paper and the Coffee Cup relative to Glen Elg's hands and the table.

- Prop 1 states that if Glen Elg held the Sports Paper with his right hand, then the Sports Paper would be to the left of the Victim's Prescription Bag.
- Prop 3 states that if Glen Elg is reading the Sports Paper, then the Sports Paper is in front of and near to Glen Elg.
- Prop 4 states that if the Coffee Cup is to the right of the Victim's Prescription Bag, then Glen Elg uses his left hand to hold the table.
- Prop 8 states that if the Sports Paper is to the left of the Victim's Prescription Bag, then Glen Elg uses his right hand to hold the table.

**Step 4:** **If none of the listed propositions apply,** explain the spatial reasoning used to identify the contradiction

Based on Kudo's testimony (Testimony 1), Glen Elg held the Sports Paper in his right hand. According to Prop 1, this means the Sports Paper should be to the left of the Victim's Prescription Bag. However, Prop 4 indicates that if the Coffee Cup is to the right of the Victim's Prescription Bag, Glen Elg uses his left hand to hold the table. Since Kudo also testified that Glen Elg picked up the Coffee Cup with his free hand (Testimony 4), this implies he used his left hand to hold the Coffee Cup.

Given these two propositions, if the Sports Paper is to the left of the Victim's Prescription Bag, Glen Elg should use his right hand to hold the table (Prop 8). But since the Coffee Cup is to the right of the Victim's Prescription Bag, he should use his left hand to hold the table (Prop 4).

This creates a contradiction because Glen Elg cannot simultaneously use both his right and left hands to hold the table while also holding the Sports Paper in his right hand and the Coffee Cup in his left hand.

**You must only answer ONE PAIR.**

## ANALYSIS FRAMEWORK:
Please follow this format:

**Step 2:** **Check if any spatial propositions** from the list above apply to this contradiction

**Step 3:** **If applicable propositions exist,** explicitly state which proposition(s) and explain how they reveal the contradiction

**Step 4:** **If none of the listed propositions apply,** explain the spatial reasoning used to identify the contradiction

**Step 5:** **Provide your answer in JSON format**

**Your answer must end with a JSON format like so:** {"evidence": 2, "testimony": 3}

**The answer JSON should strictly be the last line, with nothing before or after it.**

LLM OUTPUT (RAW RESPONSE):
------------------------------
**Step 1:** Analyze the spatial contradiction between evidences and testimonies

From the testimonies, Lamiroir states in Testimony 4 that she saw nothing because LeTouse's body was behind a wall. However, Evidence 9 indicates that the victim was lying head down on the floor, his face turned to the left, and there was smeared writing in blood on the floor in front of his head next to his right hand. This suggests that LeTouse's body was positioned in such a way that it could have been visible from the entrance door.

**Step 2:** Check if any spatial propositions from the list above apply to this contradiction

We need to check if any of the spatial propositions can help us understand the contradiction better. Propositions 1, 2, 6, 7, 8, 9, 10, 12, and 15 seem relevant to the positioning and visibility of LeTouse's body.

**Step 3:** If applicable propositions exist, explicitly state which proposition(s) and explain how they reveal the contradiction

Proposition 12 states that if the wall surrounds LeTouse, then he is enclosed by the wall. This proposition supports the idea that if LeTouse was completely surrounded by the wall, he would not be visible. However, Proposition 15 states that if LeTouse is in the same room as Lamiroir, then he can potentially see Lamiroir. Since Lamiroir was at the entrance door, and the crime scene was in her dressing room, Proposition 15 implies that LeTouse should have been visible unless he was fully enclosed by the wall.

**Step 4:** If none of the listed propositions apply, explain the spatial reasoning used to identify the contradiction

Given that Proposition 15 suggests potential visibility and Proposition 12 suggests enclosure, the contradiction arises from the fact that Lamiroir claims she saw nothing (implying LeTouse was not visible), while the crime photo (Evidence 9) suggests a position that could be visible from the entrance door. This implies that either the wall did not fully enclose LeTouse, or Lamiroir's statement about seeing nothing is false.

According to Evidence 6, the entry point of the bullet was the lower right abdomen and the exit point was the right shoulder, suggesting that the bullet traveled from a lower position to a higher position within the body.

We can check Propositions 1, 2, and 3 to see if they help us identify a contradiction.

**Step 3:** **If applicable propositions exist,** explicitly state which proposition(s) and explain how they reveal the contradiction

- **Proposition 1:** If Officer Meekins shot Oliver Deacon from on top of the stage then the bullet came from a downward direction.
- **Proposition 2:** If Oliver Deacon was shot from a downward direction then the trajectory of the bullet went from high to low.
- **Proposition 3:** If Oliver Deacon was shot from the lower right abdomen through the right shoulder then he fell backward.

However, the bullet's path as described in Evidence 6 goes from low (lower right abdomen) to high (right shoulder), which contradicts the expected trajectory from a downward shot.

**Step 2:** **Check if any spatial propositions** from the list above apply to this contradiction
